Making the new single-echo pulses 30 us long

None of the four unoptimized 30 us variants meets the prior contrast and amplitude-preservation requirements.

Both recent candidates are tested because the higher-contrast pulse was 16.18 us and the narrower phase-shaped pulse was 17.52 us. The same waveform is scaled in amplitude over 5–60 MHz; its coefficients are never retuned per amplitude. These are local simulations.

Two distinct changes: “Extend duration only” keeps core, tail, phase, transition and taper-width parameters fixed and moves the outer endpoints. “Stretch full shape” rescales every raw-envelope and phase time by 30/L, including the transition and taper; DRAG is recomputed with the rescaled derivative. Both are normalized by the maximum of the completed complex drive over both time halves. The nominal actual peak is 50 MHz. No optimization is performed.

Model: T1=51.24 us, T2=7.31 us, alpha/2pi=−216 MHz, beta=−0.5. Maps contain 111 amplitudes × 401 detunings (0.5 MHz and 2.5 kHz steps). The 50 MHz entries use 801 detunings and a halved timestep. The T2 reference is 43.54 kHz.

“Prior requirements” means contrast >0.4 at 50 MHz and no increase in linewidth or decrease in contrast versus the original polynomial at any sampled amplitude. Reference rows are the previously validated short pulses. FWHM and primary contrast use the same fitted-background metric as before; local shoulder depth is separate. Exact no-regression checks are sampled tests, not interval proofs. Coherent widths remove dissipation while keeping the same pulse; widths are not additive.
